  "content": "1: # Token - A Multi-Cursor Text Editor\n2: \n3: **Multi-cursor, code editor inspired by JetBrains IDEs, Vibe-coded in Rust, using Amp Code.**\n4: <br>\n5: Most of the threads, prompts and conversations with the agent is available to view on\n6: my [Amp profile](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre).\n7: \n8: ![Rust](https://img.shields.io/badge/Rust-000000.svg?logo=rust&logoColor=white&style=flat)\n9: [![Amp](https://img.shields.io/badge/Amp%20Code-191C19.svg?logo=data:image/svg%2bxml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMjEiIGhlaWdodD0iMjEiIHZpZXdCb3g9IjAgMCAyMSAyMSIgZmlsbD0ibm9uZSIgeG1sbnM9Imh0dHA6Ly93d3cudzMub3JnLzIwMDAvc3ZnIj4KPHBhdGggZD0iTTMuNzY4NzkgMTguMzAxNUw4LjQ5ODM5IDEzLjUwNUwxMC4yMTk2IDIwLjAzOTlMMTIuNzIgMTkuMzU2MUwxMC4yMjg4IDkuODY3NDlMMC44OTA4NzYgNy4zMzg0NEwwLjIyNTk0IDkuODkzMzFMNi42NTEzNCAxMS42Mzg4TDEuOTQxMzggMTYuNDI4MkwzLjc2ODc5IDE4LjMwMTVaIiBmaWxsPSIjRjM0RTNGIi8+CjxwYXRoIGQ9Ik0xNy40MDc0IDEyLjc0MTRMMTkuOTA3OCAxMi4wNTc1TDE3LjQxNjcgMi41Njg5N0w4LjA3ODczIDAuMDM5OTI0Nkw3LjQxMzggMi41OTQ4TDE1LjI5OTIgNC43MzY4NUwxNy40MDc0IDEyLjc0MTRaIiBmaWxsPSIjRjM0RTNGIi8+CjxwYXRoIGQ9Ik0xMy44MTg0IDE2LjM4ODNMMTYuMzE4OCAxNS43MDQ0TDEzLjgyNzYgNi4yMTU4OEw0LjQ4OTcxIDMuNjg2ODNMMy44MjQ3NyA2LjI0MTcxTDExLjcxMDEgOC4zODM3NkwxMy44MTg0IDE2LjM4ODNaIiBmaWxsPSIjRjM0RTNGIi8+Cjwvc3ZnPg==&style=flat)](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre)\n10: ![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-007ACC.svg?style=flat)\n11: [![token-editor.com](https://img.shields.io/badge/🌐_token--editor.com-007ACC?style=flat)](https://token-editor.com)\n12: \n13: <img src=\"assets/screenshot-v2-pretty.png\" alt=\"Token Screenshot\" />\n14: \n15: ---\n16: \n17: ## Installation\n18: \n19: ### Pre-built Binaries\n20: \n21: Download the latest release for your platform from [token-editor.com](https://token-editor.com#install)\n22: or [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token/releases).\n23: \n24: ### Building from Source\n25: \n26: Requires the [Rust toolchain](https://rustup.rs/).\n27: \n28: ```bash\n29: git clone https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token\n30: cd token\n31: make setup    # Install dependencies\n32: make release  # Build optimized binary\n33: make run      # Run the editor\n34: ```\n35: \n36: For development:\n37: \n38: ```bash\n39: make dev      # Run debug build (faster compile)\n40: make watch    # Start bacon watch mode\n41: ```\n42: \n43: ---\n44: \n45: ## Quick Start\n46: \n47: 1. **Open a file**: `token path/to/file.rs` or use Cmd+O\n48: 2. **Open a folder**: `token path/to/project/` or use Cmd+Shift+O\n49: 3. **Command palette**: Cmd+Shift+A for all available commands\n50: 4. **Toggle sidebar**: Cmd+1 to show/hide the file tree\n51: \n52: ---\n53: \n54: ## Keyboard Shortcuts\n55: \n56: Common shortcuts (Cmd = Command on macOS, Ctrl on Windows/Linux):\n57: \n58: ### Files & Navigation\n59: \n60: | Action          | Shortcut    |\n61: | --------------- | ----------- |\n62: | Save            | Cmd+S       |\n63: | Open File       | Cmd+O       |\n64: | Open Folder     | Cmd+Shift+O |\n65: | Command Palette | Cmd+Shift+A |\n66: | Go to Line      | Cmd+L       |\n67: | Find/Replace    | Cmd+F       |\n68: | Toggle Sidebar  | Cmd+1       |\n69: \n70: ### Editing\n71: \n72: | Action         | Shortcut      |\n73: | -------------- | ------------- |\n74: | Undo           | Cmd+Z         |\n75: | Redo           | Cmd+Shift+Z   |\n76: | Copy/Cut/Paste | Cmd+C/X/V     |\n77: | Select All     | Cmd+A         |\n78: | Duplicate      | Cmd+D         |\n79: | Delete Line    | Cmd+Backspace |\n80: \n81: ### Multi-Cursor\n82: \n83: | Action            | Shortcut           |\n84: | ----------------- | ------------------ |\n85: | Add cursor        | Option+Click       |\n86: | Add cursor above  | Option+Option+Up   |\n87: | Add cursor below  | Option+Option+Down |\n88: | Select next match | Cmd+J              |\n89: \n90: _Note: Option+Option shortcuts are hardcoded and not remappable via keymap.yaml._\n91: \n92: ### Navigation\n93: \n94: | Action             | Shortcut            |\n95: | ------------------ | ------------------- |\n96: | Word left/right    | Option+←/→          |\n97: | Line start/end     | Cmd+←/→ or Home/End |\n98: | Document start/end | Ctrl+Home/End       |\n99: | Expand selection   | Option+↑            |\n100: | Shrink selection   | Option+↓            |\n101: \n102: For the complete keybinding reference, see [docs/KEYBINDINGS.md](docs/KEYBINDINGS.md).\n103: \n104: ---\n105: \n106: ## Configuration\n107: \n108: Configuration files are stored in `~/.config/token-editor/`:\n109: \n110: | File                 | Purpose            |\n111: | -------------------- | ------------------ |\n112: | `keymap.yaml`        | Custom keybindings |\n113: | `themes/<name>.yaml` | Custom themes      |\n114: \n115: See the documentation for details:\n116: \n117: - [Keybindings](docs/KEYBINDINGS.md) — Customize keyboard shortcuts\n118: - [Themes](docs/THEMES.md) — Create and customize themes\n119: \n120: ---\n121: \n122: ## Commands\n123: \n124: Run `make help` for the full command list.\n125: \n126: | Command        | Description                      |\n127: | -------------- | -------------------------------- |\n128: | `make build`   | Build debug binary               |\n129: | `make release` | Build optimized release binary   |\n130: | `make run`     | Run release build                |\n131: | `make dev`     | Run debug build (faster compile) |\n132: | `make test`    | Run all tests                    |\n133: | `make fmt`     | Format code                      |\n134: | `make lint`    | Run clippy lints                 |\n135: \n136: ---\n137: \n138: ## Built with AI\n139: \n140: Token was built primarily through conversations with AI coding assistants, demonstrating effective human-AI\n141: collaboration on complex software projects. The development process, methodology, and all 100+ conversation threads are\n142: documented publicly.\n143: \n144: - **[Building with AI](docs/BUILDING_WITH_AI.md)** — The framework used to build Token\n145: - **[Amp Code Profile](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre)** — View the conversation threads\n146: \n147: ---\n148: \n149: ## Documentation\n150: \n151: | Document                                              | Description                             |\n152: | ----------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |\n153: | [KEYBINDINGS.md](docs/KEYBINDINGS.md)                 | Complete keyboard shortcuts reference   |\n154: | [THEMES.md](docs/THEMES.md)                           | Theme customization guide               |\n155: | [BUILDING_WITH_AI.md](docs/BUILDING_WITH_AI.md)       | AI-assisted development framework       |\n156: | [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) | Technical reference for editor geometry |\n157: | [ROADMAP.md](docs/ROADMAP.md)                         | Planned features                        |\n158: | [CHANGELOG.md](docs/CHANGELOG.md)                     | Version history                         |\n159: | [CONTRIBUTING.md](docs/CONTRIBUTING.md)               | Contribution guidelines                 |\n160: \n161: ---\n162: \n163: ## License\n164: \n165: This project is licensed under the [MIT License](LICENSE.md).\n166: \n167: The included font, [JetBrains Mono](assets/JetBrainsMono.ttf), is licensed under the [OFL-1.1](assets/OFL.txt).",

  "content": "1: # Roadmap\n2: \n3: Planned features and improvements for rust-editor.\n4: \n5: For completed work, see [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md).\n6: \n7: ---\n8: \n9: ## Recently Completed\n10: \n11: ### Workspace Management Improvements ✅\n12: \n13: **Design:** [feature/workspace-management.md](archived/workspace-management.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-17 (v0.3.7)\n14: \n15: Sidebar resize, file tree keyboard navigation, and focus management:\n16: \n17: - **Sidebar resize drag** - Click-and-drag to resize sidebar width with proper cursor feedback\n18: - **File tree keyboard navigation:**\n19:   - Arrow Up/Down to navigate between items\n20:   - Arrow Right expands folders or moves to next item\n21:   - Arrow Left collapses folders or jumps to parent folder\n22:   - Enter opens files or toggles folders\n23:   - Space toggles folder expansion\n24:   - Escape returns focus to editor\n25: - **Focus management system:**\n26:   - New `FocusTarget` enum: `Editor`, `Sidebar`, `Modal`\n27:   - Explicit focus tracking in `UiState.focus`\n28:   - Click transfers focus appropriately; modals capture/release on open/close\n29:   - Global shortcuts (Cmd+Shift+A, Cmd+S, etc.) work regardless of focus state\n30: - **Cursor icon cleanup** - I-beam only over editable text, default pointer elsewhere\n31: - **Workspace root display** - Root folder shown as first item, auto-expanded on open\n32: - **New commands:** `FileTreeSelectPrevious`, `FileTreeSelectNext`, `FileTreeOpenOrToggle`, `FileTreeRefresh`\n33: - **New messages:** `WorkspaceMsg::SelectParent` for parent folder navigation\n34: \n35: Remaining: Phase 7 (file system watching), Phase 8 (tab integration, preview tabs, open file highlighting)\n36: \n37: ### CSV Viewer/Editor Phases 1-2 ✅\n38: \n39: **Design:** [feature/csv-editor.md](archived/csv-editor.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-16 (v0.3.6)\n40: \n41: Spreadsheet-like view for CSV/TSV/PSV files with grid rendering, cell navigation, and editing:\n42: \n43: **Phase 1 (Read-Only):**\n44: - **Module structure:** `src/csv/` with model.rs, parser.rs, viewport.rs, navigation.rs, render.rs\n45: - **Data model:** `CsvData` with memory-efficient row storage (0xFA delimiter), `CsvState`, `CellPosition`\n46: - **Parsing:** RFC 4180 compliant via `csv` crate, auto-detect delimiter from content or extension\n47: - **ViewMode enum:** Added to `EditorState` for switching between Text and Csv modes\n48: - **Grid rendering:** Row numbers, column headers (A, B, C...), cell grid, selected cell highlight\n49: - **Navigation:** Arrow keys, Tab/Shift+Tab, Page Up/Down, Home/End, Cmd+Home/End\n50: - **Theme support:** `CsvTheme` with header, grid lines, selection, and number colors\n51: - **Command integration:** \"Toggle CSV View\" in command palette\n52: \n53: **Phase 2 (Cell Editing):**\n54: - **Cell editing:** Enter or typing starts editing the selected cell\n55: - **Edit buffer:** `CellEditState` tracks buffer, cursor position, original value\n56: - **Cursor navigation:** Left/Right arrows, Home/End within cell editor\n57: - **Edit operations:** Insert characters, Backspace, Delete\n58: - **Confirm/Cancel:** Enter confirms and moves down, Tab moves to next cell, Escape cancels\n59: - **Document sync:** Edits update underlying text buffer with proper CSV escaping (RFC 4180)\n60: - **Quoted fields:** Values with delimiters, quotes, or newlines properly escaped\n61: \n62: Remaining:\n63: - **Phase 3:** Copy support, virtual scrolling for large files, row/column insertion/deletion\n64: \n65: ### HiDPI Display Switching Fixes ✅\n66: \n67: **Design:** [archived/UI-SCALING-REVIEW.md](archived/UI-SCALING-REVIEW.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-16 (v0.3.4)\n68: \n69: Fixed critical issues when switching between displays with different DPI/scale factors:\n70: \n71: - **Surface resize on display change** - The softbuffer Surface is now explicitly resized after creation\n72: - **Buffer bounds checking** - `Frame::new` validates buffer size matches dimensions, preventing panics\n73: - **Dynamic tab bar height** - Computed from glyph metrics (`line_height + padding * 2`) instead of hardcoded\n74: - **Scaled metrics throughout** - `resize()` and `set_char_width()` use properly scaled metrics\n75: - **Viewport calculation** - Now accounts for tab bar height when computing visible lines\n76: \n77: Key implementation:\n78: - `ScaledMetrics` struct with all DPI-aware layout constants\n79: - `recompute_tab_bar_height_from_line_height()` for font-metric-based sizing\n80: - `Renderer::with_scale_factor()` explicitly resizes Surface after creation\n81: - `ScaleFactorChanged` triggers `Cmd::Batch([ReinitializeRenderer, Redraw])`\n82: \n83: ### Benchmark Suite Improvements ✅\n84: \n85: **Design:** [feature/benchmark-improvements.md](archived/benchmark-improvements.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-15\n86: \n87: Comprehensive audit and improvement of the benchmark suite in `benches/`:\n88: \n89: - **Fixed inaccurate benchmarks:**\n90:   - Rewrote `glyph_cache.rs` to use actual fontdue rasterization\n91:   - Created shared `token::rendering::blend_pixel_u8` function\n92:   - Removed duplicated blend code from `rendering.rs` and `support.rs`\n93: - **Added multi-cursor benchmarks:** Setup, insert, delete, move, select word (10-500 cursors)\n94: - **Added large file scaling:** 100k, 500k, 1M line tests in `rope_operations.rs`\n95: - **New `benches/search.rs`:** Literal search, case-insensitive, whole word, visible range\n96: - **New `benches/layout.rs`:** Line width, visible lines, char position, viewport layout\n97: - **New Makefile targets:** `bench-loop`, `bench-search`, `bench-layout`, `bench-multicursor`, `bench-large`\n98: \n99: Remaining: Phase 3 (criterion throughput metrics for CI), syntax highlighting benchmarks (when feature ready).\n100: \n101: ### Syntax Highlighting MVP ✅\n102: \n103: **Design:** [feature/syntax-highlighting.md](archived/syntax-highlighting.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-15\n104: \n105: Tree-sitter based syntax highlighting with async background parsing:\n106: \n107: - **Core data structures:** `HighlightToken`, `LineHighlights`, `SyntaxHighlights`, `LanguageId`\n108: - **Async parser:** Background worker thread with mpsc channels\n109: - **Debouncing:** 30ms timer prevents parsing on every keystroke\n110: - **Revision tracking:** Staleness checks discard outdated parse results\n111: - **Languages:** 17 languages supported:\n112:   - Phase 1: YAML, Markdown, Rust\n113:   - Phase 2: HTML, CSS, JavaScript\n114:   - Phase 3: TypeScript, TSX, JSON, TOML\n115:   - Phase 4: Python, Go, PHP\n116:   - Phase 5: C, C++, Java, Bash\n117: - **Theme integration:** `SyntaxTheme` struct with VS Code-like default colors\n118: - **Rendering:** Highlighted text rendering with proper tab expansion\n119: - **Auto-trigger:** Parsing on document load and content changes\n120: - **No FOUC:** Old highlights preserved until new ones arrive\n121: \n122: **v0.3.3 - Phase 3-5 languages:**\n123: - Added TypeScript/TSX with `tree-sitter-typescript` (0.23)\n124: - Added JSON with `tree-sitter-json` (0.24)\n125: - Added TOML with `tree-sitter-toml-ng` (0.7)\n126: - Added Python with `tree-sitter-python` (0.25) using built-in queries\n127: - Added Go with `tree-sitter-go` (0.25) using built-in queries\n128: - Added PHP with `tree-sitter-php` (0.24) using built-in queries\n129: - Added C with `tree-sitter-c` (0.24) using built-in queries\n130: - Added C++ with `tree-sitter-cpp` (0.23) using built-in queries\n131: - Added Java with `tree-sitter-java` (0.23) using built-in queries\n132: - Added Bash with `tree-sitter-bash` (0.25) using built-in queries\n133: - Upgraded tree-sitter core from 0.24 to 0.25 (ABI compatibility)\n134: - 671 total tests passing\n135: \n136: **Performance improvements (v0.3.2):**\n137: - Implemented proper incremental parsing with `tree.edit()` and tree caching\n138: - `ParserState` caches trees per document for incremental updates\n139: - `compute_incremental_edit()` diffs old/new source to generate `InputEdit`\n140: - Added comprehensive benchmark suite (`benches/syntax.rs`)\n141: - Parse times: 67µs (100 lines) to 7.5ms (10000 lines)\n142: \n143: **Bug fixes (v0.3.1):**\n144: - Fixed tree-sitter incremental parsing bug causing misaligned highlights\n145: - Fixed flash of unstyled text during re-parsing\n146: - Fixed tab expansion in highlight token rendering\n147: \n148: Future phases: Language injection for PHP/HTML/Vue, semantic highlighting via LSP.\n149: \n150: ### File Operations – Phases 1-5 Complete ✅\n151: \n152: **Design:** [feature/file-operations.md](archived/file-operations.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-15\n153: \n154: Comprehensive file handling with dialogs, CLI arguments, and drag-drop feedback:\n155: \n156: - **Phase 1:** Refactored `AppModel::new()` with `ViewportGeometry`, `load_config_and_theme()`, `create_initial_session()` helpers\n157: - **Phase 2:** Native file dialogs via `rfd` crate (⌘O Open File, ⇧⌘O Open Folder, ⇧⌘S Save As)\n158: - **Phase 3:** Visual feedback for file drag-hover (`DropState`, overlay rendering)\n159: - **Phase 4:** File validation (`FileOpenError`, binary detection, 50MB size limit)\n160: - **Phase 5:** CLI arguments with `clap` (`--new`, `--wait`, `--line`, `--column`)\n161: - **Duplicate file detection:** Already-open files focus existing tab\n162: - Added `workspace_root: Option<PathBuf>` to `AppModel`\n163: - 703 total tests passing\n164: \n165: ### Centralized Config Paths ✅\n166: \n167: **Completed:** 2025-12-15\n168: \n169: Single source of truth for configuration directories:\n170: \n171: - New `src/config_paths.rs` module centralizing all path logic\n172: - XDG-compliant paths on Unix (`~/.config/token-editor/`)\n173: - Windows support (`%APPDATA%\\token-editor\\`)\n174: - Moved inline tests to `tests/config.rs`\n175: - Fixed command palette navigation clamping bug\n176: - 597 total tests passing\n177: \n178: ### User Theme Configuration ✅\n179: \n180: **Completed:** 2025-12-15\n181: \n182: Theme loading from user config directories with persistence:\n183: \n184: - **Layered loading**: User config → embedded builtins\n185:   - User themes at `~/.config/token-editor/themes/*.yaml`\n186:   - 4 built-in themes (default-dark, fleet-dark, github-dark, github-light)\n187: - **Config persistence**: Theme selection saved to `~/.config/token-editor/config.yaml`\n188: - **ThemePicker improvements**: Sectioned list showing User/Builtin themes\n189: - **New APIs**: `load_theme(id)`, `list_available_themes()`, `ThemeInfo`, `ThemeSource`\n190: - **EditorConfig**: New config module for persistent editor settings\n191: - **Fallback handling**: Falls back to default-dark if saved theme not found\n192: - 15 theme tests, 556 total tests passing\n193: \n194: ### Configurable Keymapping System ✅\n195: \n196: **Design:** [archived/KEYMAPPING_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](archived/KEYMAPPING_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-15\n197: \n198: Data-driven keybinding system with YAML configuration:\n199: \n200: - **Core module** (`src/keymap/`): Types, commands, bindings, context, YAML parser\n201: - **74 default bindings** in `keymap.yaml` (embedded at compile time)\n202: - **Platform-aware `cmd` modifier**: Maps to Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere\n203: - **Context-aware bindings**: `when: [\"has_selection\"]` for conditional activation\n204: - **User configuration**: Layered loading from embedded → user config\n205:   - User keymap at `~/.config/token-editor/keymap.yaml`\n206:   - `merge_bindings()` combines base + user with override semantics\n207:   - `command: Unbound` to disable default bindings\n208: - **Bridge integration**: Keymap tried first, input.rs fallback for complex behaviors\n209: - **Chord infrastructure**: `KeyAction::AwaitMore` for multi-key sequences\n210: - **Cleanup**: input.rs reduced 54% (477→220 lines), removed all redundant match arms\n211: - Tab behavior: Indent with selection, insert tab without\n212: - Escape cascade: Multi-cursor → selection → nothing\n213: - Option double-tap preserved for multi-cursor gestures\n214: - 74 keymap tests, 546 total tests passing\n215: \n216: ### GUI Phase 1 – Frame/Painter Abstraction ✅\n217: \n218: **Design:** [GUI-CLEANUP.md](archived/GUI-CLEANUP.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-08\n219: \n220: Centralized drawing primitives for cleaner rendering code:\n221: \n222: - `Frame` struct wraps pixel buffer with safe drawing methods\n223: - `TextPainter` struct wraps fontdue + glyph cache for text\n224: - All render functions migrated (`render_*_static`, `render_perf_overlay`)\n225: - Removed legacy `draw_text()` and `draw_sparkline()` functions\n226: - Next: Phase 2 (Widget Extraction) or Phase 3 (Modal/Focus System)\n227: \n228: ### Debug Tracing & Instrumentation ✅\n229: \n230: **Design:** [tracing-instrumentation.md](archived/tracing-instrumentation.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-08\n231: \n232: Debug instrumentation for multi-cursor state transitions:\n233: \n234: - `tracing` crate replaces `log`/`env_logger`\n235: - `CursorSnapshot` captures before/after state with diffing\n236: - `update_traced()` wrapper logs message flow and cursor changes\n237: - `assert_invariants_with_context()` for contextual assertion failures\n238: - F8 toggle for in-editor debug overlay\n239: - `make trace` runs with `RUST_LOG=debug`\n240: - Human-readable message names (e.g., `Editor::MoveCursor(Up)` instead of discriminants)\n241: \n242: ### Multi-Cursor Line Operations ✅\n243: \n244: **Completed:** 2025-12-07\n245: \n246: Fixed line-based operations to work with all cursors:\n247: \n248: - **IndentLines** - now indents lines at all cursor positions\n249: - **UnindentLines** - now unindents lines at all cursor positions\n250: - **DeleteLine** - now deletes lines at all cursor positions\n251: - **AddCursorAbove/Below** - now expands from edge cursors, not primary\n252: - Uses `lines_covered_by_all_cursors()` helper for unique line collection\n253: - 10 new tests in `tests/multi_cursor.rs`\n254: \n255: ### Test Extraction ✅\n256: \n257: **Completed:** 2025-12-07\n258: \n259: Extracted inline tests from production code to `tests/` folder:\n260: \n261: - `tests/editor_area.rs` - 7 tests (Rect, layout, hit testing)\n262: - `tests/overlay.rs` - 7 tests (anchor positioning, alpha blending)\n263: - `tests/theme.rs` - 10 tests (Color parsing, YAML themes, builtins)\n264: - Tests in `src/main.rs` (33) remain - they test binary-only `handle_key()`\n265: \n266: ### Codebase Organization ✅\n267: \n268: **Design:** [archived/ORGANIZATION-CODEBASE.md](archived/ORGANIZATION-CODEBASE.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-06\n269: \n270: Restructured large files for maintainability:\n271: \n272: - Converted `update.rs` (2900 lines) → `update/` module directory with 5 submodules\n273: - Extracted from `main.rs`: `view.rs`, `app.rs`, `input.rs`, `perf.rs`\n274: - `main.rs` now ~20 lines (entry point) + tests\n275: - `update/mod.rs` is a pure 36-line dispatcher\n276: \n277: ### Multi-Cursor Selection Gaps ✅\n278: \n279: **Design:** [archived/MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md](archived/MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-06\n280: \n281: Fixed remaining selection operations to work with multiple cursors:\n282: \n283: - `merge_overlapping_selections()` - merge overlapping/touching selections\n284: - `SelectWord` - select word at each cursor with automatic merge\n285: - `SelectLine` - select line at each cursor with automatic merge\n286: - `SelectAll` - properly collapses to single full-document selection\n287: - `ExtendSelectionToPosition` - collapses multi-cursor first, then extends\n288: \n289: ### Expand/Shrink Selection ✅\n290: \n291: **Design:** [archived/TEXT-SHRINK-EXPAND-SELECTION.md](archived/TEXT-SHRINK-EXPAND-SELECTION.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-06\n292: \n293: Progressive selection expansion with history stack:\n294: \n295: - Option+Up: Expand (cursor → word → line → all)\n296: - Option+Down: Shrink (restore previous from history)\n297: - 18 tests in `tests/expand_shrink_selection.rs`\n298: \n299: ### Multi-Cursor Movement ✅\n300: \n301: **Design:** [archived/MULTI_CURSOR_MOVEMENT.md](archived/MULTI_CURSOR_MOVEMENT.md) | **Completed:** 2025-12-06\n302: \n303: All cursor movement operations now work with multiple cursors:\n304: \n305: - Arrow keys, Home/End, Word navigation, Page Up/Down move ALL cursors\n306: - Shift+movement extends selection for ALL cursors\n307: - Cursor deduplication when cursors collide\n308: - Each cursor preserves its own `desired_column`\n309: - 10 new tests in `tests/cursor_movement.rs`\n310: \n311: ---\n312: \n313: ## Planned Features\n314: \n315: ### Workspace Management - Remaining Phases\n316: \n317: **Design:** [feature/workspace-management.md](archived/workspace-management.md)\n318: \n319: Phases 0-6 complete. permanent)\n330: - Highlight open files in tree\n331: - Sync tree selection with active tab\n332: - Support opening files in new split pane\n333: \n334: ### Command Palette & Modal System\n335: \n336: VS Code-style command palette and modal overlays:\n337: \n338: - `Cmd+Shift+P` opens command palette\n339: - Fuzzy search over all available commands\n340: - Go to Line, Find/Replace as modal dialogs\n341: - Focus capture for modal key routing\n342: - Builds on existing overlay system\n343: \n344: ### GUI Architecture Improvements\n345: \n346: **Design:** [GUI-CLEANUP.md](archived/GUI-CLEANUP.md)\n347: \n348: Thin, editor-focused view layer abstractions:\n349: \n350: - Frame/Painter abstraction for drawing primitives ✅\n351: - TextPainter for text rendering ✅\n352: - Widget extraction (tab bar, gutter, text area, status bar)\n353: - Centralized coordinate space conversions\n354: - Keep existing winit/softbuffer/fontdue stack\n355: \n356: ### Undo Coalescing\n357: \n358: Group rapid consecutive edits into single undo entries:\n359: \n360: - Time-based grouping (e.g., 300ms threshold)\n361: - Coalesce consecutive character insertions\n362: - Break on cursor movement or pause\n363: - Improves undo ergonomics for normal typing\n364: \n365: ---\n366: \n367: ## Future Enhancements\n368: \n369: | Feature | Design Doc |\n370: Opening preview on `.html` files now displays the rendered HTML in the webview pane, reusing the existing markdown preview infrastructure.\n19: - Preview: Local resource loading for HTML preview. Images, CSS, and JS files are now served from the document's directory via a custom `token://` protocol handler.\n20: - Preview: Preview now updates to show the new file when switching tabs (if the new file supports preview), instead of closing.\n21: - API: Added `LanguageId::supports_preview()` method to check if a file type supports live preview.\n22: - API: Added `content_to_preview_html()` function that handles both Markdown and HTML content.\n23: \n24: ---\n25: \n26: ## v0.3.14 - 2026-01-09\n27: \n28: ### Added\n29: - Docs: Updated developer OVERVIEW and archived obsolete docs to reflect recent refactors and module moves.\n30: - Tests: Comprehensive damage computation tests added for redraw/damage logic.\n31: \n32: ### Fixed\n33: - Layout: Editor viewport now correctly shrinks to accommodate dock panels (bottom/right). Fixed issue where dock panels would obscure part of the editor area because the logical viewport didn't account for dock sizes.\n34: - Layout: Viewport dimensions now recalculated when dock visibility or size changes.\n35: - Preview: Fixed dual rendering issue where both native markdown and webview were drawn. Native rendering is now properly disabled when webview is active.\n36: - Preview: Fixed webview misalignment by correctly converting physical pixel coordinates to logical points with proper Y-axis flipping for macOS.\n37: - Scroll: Scroll events over dock panels and preview panes are now properly consumed instead of bleeding into the editor.\n38: - Rendering: Minor fixes to selection/cursor rendering behavior related to the cursor-blink optimization and partial redraws.\n39: - Sidebar: Fixed syntax highlighting not triggering when opening files via sidebar double-click. Commands from update() calls in mouse handlers are now properly propagated.\n40: \n41: ### Changed\n42: - Refactor (mouse): Unified mouse event handling with new hit-test system in `src/view/hit_test.rs` and centralized dispatch in `src/runtime/mouse.rs`. Replaces ad-hoc if/else chains with explicit `HitTarget` enum and priority-ordered hit-testing.\n43: - Refactor (app): Introduced specific redraw helpers and tighter damage accumulation to reduce unnecessary full redraws; clearer separation between damage computation and command processing.\n44: - Refactor (layout): Added `redraw_editor`-style helpers to limit redraw scope when layout changes are localized.\n45: - Refactor (ui): Optimized cursor-blink related redraw behavior to prevent selection flicker and avoid unnecessary redraws of unrelated regions.\n46: - Cleanup: Removed dead code from view module (`is_in_tab_bar`, `is_in_modal`, unused Renderer getters, wrapper functions superseded by ViewportGeometry).\n47: \n48: ---\n49: \n50: ## v0.3.13 - 2026-01-07\n51: \n52: ### Fixed - {
  "content": "1: # AI-Assisted Development: Building Token\n2: \n3: Token is a multi-cursor text editor written in Rust—around 15,000 lines of code built primarily through 116 conversations with [Amp Code](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre).\n4: \n5: This document describes the methodology that made sustained AI collaboration work on a project that doesn't fit in a single context window. It's not about what AI can do in a demo; it's about what actually works across months of development.\n6: \n7: The core insight: **AI agents excel at focused, well-defined tasks. The human's job is to provide structure—clear phases, written specifications, and explicit invariants.** The more complex your project, the more this structure pays off.\n8: \n9: Text editors are a good stress test for this approach. They look simple but hide real complexity: cursor choreography with selection, keyboard modifier edge cases across platforms, grapheme cluster handling, viewport scrolling that \"feels right.\" Getting these details consistent across dozens of AI sessions requires explicit documentation that both humans and machines can reference. See [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) for what that looks like in practice.\n10: \n11: ---\n12: \n13: ## The Framework\n14: \n15: ### Three Modes of Work\n16: \n17: Before each session, explicitly state which mode you're in:\n18: \n19: | Mode        | Purpose                                       | Inputs                       | Example                          |\n20: | ----------- | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------- |\n21: | **Build**   | New behavior that didn't exist                | Feature spec, reference docs | \"Implement split view (Phase 3)\" |\n22: | **Improve** | Better architecture without changing behavior | Organization docs, roadmap   | \"Extract modules from main.rs\"   |\n23: | **Sweep**   | Fix a cluster of related bugs                 | Bug tracker, gap doc         | \"Multi-cursor selection bugs\"    |\n24: \n25: This prevents scope creep and keeps AI contributions coherent across sessions.\n26: \n27: ---\n28: \n29: ### Design Before Code\n30: \n31: For complex features, invest in upfront documentation:\n32: \n33: **1. Reference Documentation**\n34: Create a \"document of truth\" for cross-cutting concerns. [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) defines viewport math, coordinate systems, and scrolling behavior—used across split view, selection, and overlay implementations.\n35: \n36: **2. Feature Specifications**\n37: Before implementing multi-cursor, we wrote [SELECTION_MULTICURSOR.md](archived/SELECTION_MULTICURSOR.md):\n38: \n39: - Data structures and invariants\n40: - Keyboard shortcuts table\n41: - Message enums and expected behavior\n42: - Multi-phase implementation plan\n43: \n44: **3. Gap Documents**\n45: When a feature is 60-90% complete, create a gap doc. [MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md](archived/MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md) lists:\n46: \n47: - What's implemented vs. missing\n48: - Design decisions for each gap\n49: - Tests and success criteria\n50: \n51: This turns \"vague incompleteness\" into concrete, actionable tasks.\n52: \n53: ---\n54: \n55: ### The Multi-Cursor Migration: A Case Study\n56: \n57: Adding multi-cursor to a single-cursor editor touched nearly every file. Here's how we avoided chaos:\n58: \n59: **1. Wrote invariants upfront:**\n60: \n61: ```rust\n62: // MUST maintain: cursors.len() == selections.len()\n63: // MUST maintain: cursors[i].to_position() == selections[i].head\n64: ```\n65: \n66: **2. Created migration helpers:**\n67: \n68: ```rust\n69: // Old code still works via accessor\n70: impl AppModel {\n71:     pub fn cursor(&self) -> &Cursor { &self.editor.cursors[0] }\n72: }\n73: ```\n74: \n75: **3. Implemented in phases:**\n76: \n77: - Phase 0: Per-cursor primitives (`move_cursor_left_at(idx)`)\n78: - Phase 1: All-cursor wrappers (`move_all_cursors_left()`)\n79: - Phase 2-4: Update handlers, add tests\n80: - Phase 5: Bug sweep for edge cases\n81: \n82: **4. Ran targeted sweeps:**\n83: When bugs emerged, we created a focused tracker and fixed them systematically—not one-off. This leverages AI's strength at \"apply this pattern everywhere.\"\n84: \n85: ---\n86: \n87: ### Agent Configuration\n88: \n89: Tell agents how to work in your codebase:\n90: \n91: | File        | Purpose                                         |\n92: | ----------- | ----------------------------------------------- |\n93: | `AGENTS.md` | Build commands, architecture, conventions       |\n94: | `CLAUDE.md` | Same (symlink or duplicate for different tools) |\n95: \n96: Key: specify your Makefile/scripts so agents use `make test` instead of inventing `cargo test --all-features --no-fail-fast`.\n97: \n98: ---\n99: \n100: ### Documentation Structure\n101: \n102: | Path                                             | Purpose                                             |\n103: | ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------- |\n104: | [docs/ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md)                    | Planned features with design doc links              |\n105: | [docs/CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md)                | Completed work (reference when things break)        |\n106: | [docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) | Domain reference (geometry, coordinates, scrolling) |\n107: | `docs/feature/`                                  | Design specs for planned features                   |\n108: | `docs/archived/`                                 | Completed feature specs (kept for reference)        |\n109: \n110: ---\n111: \n112: ### Agent Workflow Patterns\n113: \n114: **Research → Synthesize → Implement:**\n115: \n116: 1. Use **Librarian** to research how VSCode/Zed/Helix solve a problem\n117: 2. Use **Oracle** to review findings and produce a design doc\n118: 3. Implement in phases with tests\n119: \n120: **Review Before Implementation:**\n121: Having Oracle review [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) caught 15+ issues (off-by-one errors, division-by-zero edge cases) before they became bugs in code.\n122: \n123: ---\n124: \n125: ## Development Timeline\n126: \n127: Token's development followed distinct phases, each with focused objectives:\n128: \n129: | Phase                | Dates          | Focus                                                 |\n130: | -------------------- | -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- |\n131: | Foundation           | Sep 26 - Dec 5 | Setup, reference docs, architecture                   |\n132: | Research Sprint      | Dec 6          | Performance, keymaps, testing infrastructure          |\n133: | Feature Development  | Dec 5-6        | Split view, undo/redo, multi-cursor selection         |\n134: | Codebase Refactor    | Dec 6          | Extract modules from main.rs (3100→20 lines)          |\n135: | Research & Polish    | Dec 7          | Zed research, cursor API fixes, test extraction       |\n136: | Maintenance          | Dec 7          | Bugfixes, benchmarks, documentation                   |\n137: | Keymapping System    | Dec 15         | Configurable YAML keybindings, 74 default bindings    |\n138: | Syntax Highlighting  | Dec 15         | Tree-sitter integration, 17 languages supported       |\n139: | CSV Viewer/Editor    | Dec 16         | Spreadsheet view with cell editing (Phases 1-2)       |\n140: | HiDPI Fixes          | Dec 16         | Display switching, ScaledMetrics system               |\n141: | Workspace Management | Dec 17         | Sidebar file tree, focus system, global shortcuts     |\n142: | Unified Text Editing | Dec 19         | EditableState system, modal/CSV clipboard & selection |\n143: \n144: ---\n145: \n146: ## Notable Threads\n147: \n148: <details>\n149: <summary><strong>Oracle: UI Reference Deep Review</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-7b92a860-a2f7-4397-985c-73b2fa3e9582\">T-7b92a860</a></summary>\n150: \n151: **Date**: 2025-12-03\n152: \n153: The Oracle performed a comprehensive technical review of EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md and identified **15+ issues**:\n154: \n155: **Critical Bugs Found**:\n156: \n157: - Off-by-one error in viewport calculations: `lastVisibleLine` should be `firstVisibleLine + visibleLines - 1`\n158: - Division-by-zero edge cases in scrollbar thumb calculations\n159: - `preferredColumn` was documented as column index but code used pixel X values\n160: \n161: **Semantic Issues**:\n162: \n163: - Selection struct uses `anchor/head` internally but docs said `start/end`\n164: - Missing coverage for folding, soft-wrap interaction, IME composition, BiDi text\n165: \n166: **Impact**: Created systematic AMP_REPORT.md with prioritized fixes. Prevented 1-3h of debugging per issue.\n167: \n168: **Lesson Learned**: Always have oracle review reference docs before implementation - catches subtle algorithmic bugs that tests miss.\n169: \n170: </details>\n171: \n172: <details>\n173: <summary><strong>Librarian: Keymap System Design</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-35b11d40-96b0-4177-9c75-4c723dfd8f80\">T-35b11d40</a></summary>\n174: \n175: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n176: \n177: **Research Question**: How do major editors implement configurable keyboard mappings?\n178: \n179: **Projects Studied**:\n180: | Editor | Pattern | Key Insight |\n181: |--------|---------|-------------|\n182: | VSCode | Flat vector + context | User overrides predictable via insertion order |\n183: | Helix | Trie-based | Efficient for prefix matching in modal editors |\n184: | Zed | Flat with depth indexing | Context depth + insertion order for precedence |\n185: | Neovim | Lua-based | Full scripting for complex mappings |\n186: \n187: **Key Finding**: Two dominant patterns - **trie-based** for modal editors (Helix) vs **flat vector with indexing** for context-rich editors (Zed, VSCode).\n188: \n189: **Outcome**: Created [docs/feature/KEYMAPPING.md](archived/KEYMAPPING.md) design doc with data structures, TOML config format, and phased implementation plan.\n190: \n191: </details>\n192: \n193: <details>\n194: <summary><strong>Critical Bug: Cmd+Z Was Typing 'z'!</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-519a8c9d-b94f-45e5-98e0-5bfc34c77cbf\">T-519a8c9d</a></summary>\n195: \n196: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n197: \n198: **The Bug**: Undo/redo keyboard shortcuts were completely broken on macOS. Pressing Cmd+Z inserted the letter 'z' into the document instead of undoing!\n199: \n200: **Root Cause**: Key handler only checked `control_key()` modifier, not `super_key()` (macOS Command key).\n201: \n202: **The Fix**:\n203: \n204: ```rust\n205: // Before (broken on macOS)\n206: if modifiers.control_key() && key == \"z\" { ... }\n207: \n208: // After (cross-platform)\n209: if (modifiers.control_key() || modifiers.super_key()) && key == \"z\" { ... }\n210: ```\n211: \n212: **Lesson Learned**: Always handle both `control_key()` AND `super_key()` for cross-platform keyboard shortcuts. The macOS Command key is a completely different modifier than Ctrl.\n213: \n214: </details>\n215: \n216: <details>\n217: <summary><strong>Split View: EditorArea Architecture</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-29b1dd08-eee1-44fb-abd5-eb982d6bcd52\">T-29b1dd08</a></summary>\n218: \n219: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n220: \n221: **Problem**: Implement multi-pane editing with tabs, splits, and shared documents.\n222: \n223: **Architectural Decisions**:\n224: \n225: 1. **Shared documents, independent editors**: Documents stored in `HashMap<DocumentId, Document>`, editors in `HashMap<EditorId, EditorState>`. Multiple editors can view the same document with independent cursors/viewports.\n226: \n227: 2. **Layout tree structure**: `LayoutNode` is either `Group(GroupId)` or `Split(SplitContainer)`. Recursive structure allows arbitrary nesting.\n228: \n229: 3. **Migration helper**: `EditorArea::single_document()` provides backward compatibility:\n230: \n231: ```rust\n232: impl AppModel {\n233:     pub fn document(&self) -> &Document {\n234:         self.editor_area.focused_document()  // Hides complexity\n235:     }\n236: }\n237: ```\n238: \n239: **Lesson Learned**: Migration helpers like `single_document()` are crucial for phased refactoring. The pattern of \"replace two fields with one complex struct + accessors\" worked smoothly.\n240: \n241: </details>\n242: \n243: <details>\n244: <summary><strong>Multi-Cursor: Only Primary Cursor Moved!</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-d4c75d42-c0c1-4746-a609-593bff88db6d\">T-d4c75d42</a></summary>\n245: \n246: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n247: \n248: **The Bug**: Arrow keys and all movement operations only affected the primary cursor. Secondary cursors were frozen!\n249: \n250: **Root Cause**: All movement handlers assumed single cursor - `move_cursor_up()` operated on `cursor_mut()` which only returned `cursors[0]`.\n[... omitted lines 251 to 416 ...]\n417: \n418: **Stress Tested**: Handles 10k+ row files with virtual scrolling\n419: \n420: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b22bd, T-019b2734, T-019b274f, T-019b275d, T-019b2783, T-019b2794\n421: \n422: </details>\n423: \n424: <details>\n425: <summary><strong>Workspace Management & File Tree</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b2b7a-8dd7-763a-9ab7-3132ddcf516a\">T-019b2b7a</a></summary>\n426: \n427: **Date**: 2025-12-17\n428: \n429: **Problem**: No file browser - users had to open files via command line or dialogs.\n430: \n431: **Solution**: Full workspace management with sidebar file tree:\n432: \n433: - **File tree sidebar** with expand/collapse folders, file icons\n434: - **Focus management system**: `FocusTarget` enum (Editor, Sidebar, Modal)\n435: - **Mouse interaction**: Double-click to open files, single-click chevron for folders\n436: - **Keyboard navigation**: Arrow keys, Enter to open, Escape to return to editor\n437: - **Theming**: Full sidebar color customization in theme YAML\n438: \n439: **Architecture**:\n440: \n441: ```rust\n442: struct Workspace {\n443:     root: PathBuf,\n444:     file_tree: FileTree,\n445:     sidebar_visible: bool,\n446:     sidebar_width: f32,\n447: }\n448: ```\n449: \n450: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b2b7a, T-019b2bc5, T-019b2be7, T-019b2c40, T-019b2c8a, T-019b2ca6\n451: \n452: </details>\n453: \n454: <details>\n455: <summary><strong>HiDPI Display Scaling Fixes</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b2476-55a8-7058-9ba9-9360a9280c1b\">T-019b2476</a></summary>\n456: \n457: **Date**: 2025-12-16\n458: \n459: **Problem**: Tab area too small on Retina displays, debug overlay misaligned - scaling broken on HiDPI.\n460: \n461: **Root Cause Investigation**:\n462: \n463: - Metrics calculated in logical units but used as physical pixels\n464: - Font sizes not scaled by DPI factor\n465: - Inconsistent coordinate systems between rendering and hit testing\n466: \n467: **The Fix - ScaledMetrics**:\n468: \n469: ```rust\n470: pub struct ScaledMetrics {\n471:     pub tab_bar_height: u32,      // Pre-scaled by scale_factor\n472:     pub status_bar_height: u32,\n473:     pub line_height: u32,\n474:     pub char_width: u32,\n475:     // ... all measurements pre-scaled\n476: }\n477: ```\n478: \n479: **Key Insight**: Scale once at metrics creation, use physical pixels everywhere else.\n480: \n481: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b2476, T-019b26ca, T-019b26e5\n482: \n483: </details>\n484: \n485: <details>\n486: <summary><strong>Unified Text Editing System</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b3643-79af-7573-bb69-18dac832eb94\">T-019b3643</a></summary>\n487: \n488: **Date**: 2025-12-19\n489: \n490: **Problem**: 5 separate text editing implementations (main editor, command palette, go-to-line, find/replace, CSV cells) with duplicated logic and inconsistent behavior.\n491: \n492: **Solution**: Created unified `src/editable/` module with:\n493: \n494: ```rust\n495: // Core abstraction for all text editing\n496: pub struct EditableState<B: TextBuffer> {\n497:     pub buffer: B,                    // StringBuffer or RopeBuffer\n498:     pub cursors: Vec<Cursor>,\n499:     pub selections: Vec<Selection>,\n500:     pub constraints: EditConstraints,  // Context-specific rules\n501:     history: EditHistory,              // Undo/redo\n502: }\n503: \n504: // Unified message type\n505: pub enum TextEditMsg {\n506:     Move(MoveTarget),\n507:     MoveWithSelection(MoveTarget),\n508:     InsertChar(char),\n509:     DeleteBackward,\n510:     SelectAll,\n511:     Undo,\n512:     Redo,\n513:     // ...\n514: }\n515: ```\n516: \n517: **Key Insight**: Abstract buffer operations behind traits, share editing logic across all contexts.\n518: \n519: **Benefits**:\n520: - All modals now have full cursor navigation, selection, word movement\n521: - CSV cell editing has undo/redo, selection, clipboard support\n522: - Bridge pattern maps `TextEditMsg` to existing editor messages for gradual migration\n523: \n524: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b3643, T-019b3664, T-019b3671, T-019b367e, T-019b3688, T-019b368e, T-019b369a\n525: \n526: </details>\n527: \n528: ---\n529: \n530: ## Full Thread Reference (116 threads)\n531: \n532: All conversations are public. Vague requirements produce vague code.\n655: \n656: 2. **Modes prevent scope creep**: Explicitly declaring Build/Improve/Sweep mode at session start keeps both human and AI focused on one type of work.\n657: \n658: 3. **Gap docs are underrated**: When a feature is \"mostly done,\" a gap document transforms fuzzy incompleteness into a concrete checklist.\n659: \n660: 4. **Review docs before code**: Having an AI review reference documentation catches algorithmic bugs before they become code bugs.\n661: \n662: 5. **Per-item primitives scale**: When extending single-item code to multi-item (e.g., single cursor → multi-cursor), the \"primitive + wrapper\" pattern works reliably.\n663: \n664: 6. **Parallel arrays need invariants**: If you maintain parallel arrays (cursors, selections), document the invariant explicitly and check it often.\n665: \n666: 7. **Migration helpers enable incremental refactoring**: Accessor methods like `cursor()` that delegate to the new structure let you migrate call sites gradually.",

## TL;DR
Add a **/showcase** (screenshots gallery) first, then “low-effort / high-impact” credibility pages that you already have content for: **/changelog**, **/roadmap**, and a **/docs** hub (install/config/FAQ). These pages improve conversion (gallery), trust (changelog/roadmap), and retention (docs) without inventing new content.

## Recommended approach (simple path)

### 1) `/showcase` — screenshots & feature gallery (highest marketing impact) *(M: 1–3h)*
**Why:** People deciding on an editor want to *see it* immediately; you already have lots of screenshots.

**Structure idea:**
- Hero: “Token in action” + 1 standout screenshot
- Filterable gallery (client-side, lightweight):
  - Tags like: `Multi-cursor`, `Splits`, `CSV`, `Minimal`, `Themes`, `Sidebar`, `Preview`
- Each screenshot opens a detail modal/page with:
  - Short caption (“Multi-cursor rename across a file”, “3-way split editing”, etc.)
  - Optional “How to do this” snippet (keybinding or command palette command)
  - Link to relevant page: `/keybindings`, `/themes`, `/docs/...`

**Implementation notes (Astro-simple):**
- Put images in `public/showcase/...` (or reference existing `public/` + `screenshots/output/` via a build copy step).
- Create a small `showcase.ts` data file:
  ```ts
  export const shots = [
    { src: "/showcase/splits.png", title: "Split views", tags: ["Splits"] },
    { src: "/showcase/multicursor.png", title: "Multi-cursor editing", tags: ["Multi-cursor"] },
  ]
  ```
- Use a tiny bit of client JS for tag filtering + modal; keep it static-first.

---

### 2) `/docs` — documentation hub (quick start, install, config, FAQ) *(M: 1–3h)*
**Why:** Reduces friction after the first download and makes Token feel “real” to devs.

**Subpages (all can be Markdown-driven):**
- `/docs/install` (the curl script + manual + “how to update/uninstall”)
- `/docs/config` (config paths, config.yaml, keymap.yaml, theme files)
- `/docs/languages` (the 18 languages list + file extensions + tree-sitter note)
- `/docs/faq` (no telemetry, file size limits, platform notes, why no Electron, etc.)

**Simple content source:** reuse sections from `README.md`, `docs/KEYBINDINGS.md`, `docs/THEMES.md`.

---

### 3) `/changelog` — version history + “What’s new” *(S–M: 30–90m)*
**Why:** Changelog pages are underrated marketing: they show momentum, seriousness, and reduce perceived risk.

**Use your `CHANGELOG.md`:**
- Render as a page with anchors per version (v0.3.17 etc.)
- Add a small “Latest release” summary at the top with download CTA
- Optional: “Subscribe to releases” link (GitHub releases RSS)

---

### 4) `/roadmap` — planned features & status *(S–M: 30–90m)*
**Why:** Indie projects convert better when users see direction + transparency.

**Use `ROADMAP.md` directly:**
- Sections: “Recently completed”, “Planned”, “Deferred”
- Add “How to influence roadmap” CTA: link to GitHub issues/discussions

---

### 5) `/built-with-ai` — the methodology page (not just a link) *(M: 1–3h)*
**Why:** You already tease “Built with AI” on the landing page; turning that into a real page makes it a differentiator instead of a footnote.

**Content:**
- A short narrative summary + a “How we worked” excerpt from `BUILDING_WITH_AI.md`
- Highlight 3–5 “Notable threads” with links
- A “Start here” box for people wanting to replicate the approach

This becomes shareable on Hacker News / Rust communities.

---

## Other page ideas (pick 1–2, don’t boil the ocean)

### `/performance` — “fast, minimal, no Electron” receipts *(M: 1–3h)*
- Show cold start numbers, idle CPU behavior (you have changelog entries about event loop fixes)
- Simple charts or even “measured on X machine” tables
- Links to benchmark docs/commands (`make bench-*`) for credibility

### `/community` or `/contributing` — lightweight contributor funnel *(S–M)*
- How to build, how to run tests, where to ask questions
- Good first issues + coding conventions
- Pull from `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `AGENTS.md` patterns if public

### `/compare` — Token vs VS Code / Zed / Sublime (careful, but effective) *(M)*
- Keep it respectful and scoped: “If you want X, use Y; if you want minimal + fast + local config, Token.”
- This page often ranks in search and helps positioning.

## Rationale and trade-offs
- **Gallery + docs + changelog + roadmap** is the best “indie editor” website bundle: it covers *visual proof*, *getting started*, and *project credibility*.
- Reusing Markdown docs keeps scope small and avoids building a CMS/blog prematurely.

## Risks and guardrails
- **Too many nav items** can clutter the sleek UI. Guardrail: add only 1–2 new top-level tabs (e.g., `Showcase`, `Docs`), and put `Changelog/Roadmap/AI` under a small “More” menu or footer.
- **Large screenshots** can slow the site. Guardrail: ensure compressed images, use responsive `srcset` (Astro assets pipeline if possible), and lazy-load below the fold.

## When to consider the advanced path
- You want SEO/content growth (blog posts, tutorials), or you start getting steady organic traffic and want to convert it.

## Optional advanced path (only if needed)
- Add a `/blog` powered by Astro Content Collections:
  - Auto-generate release posts from `CHANGELOG.md` sections
  - Add “Feature deep-dives” (CSV editing, multi-cursor invariants, hit-test system) that link back to the gallery shots and docs.

If you implement just one thing next: **/showcase** with tagging + modal details will likely move the needle the most for token-editor.com.
```

#### High impact, low effort
1. **`/showcase`** — Screenshot gallery with tags (Multi-cursor, Splits, CSV, Themes) and lightbox modals. You already have the screenshots. Add a small `showcase.ts` data file like keybindings/themes.
2. **`/changelog`** — Render your `CHANGELOG.md` as a page. Shows momentum and seriousness. Quick win.
3. **`/roadmap`** — Same treatment for `ROADMAP.md`. Shows direction and transparency.

#### Medium effort, strong differentiator
4. **`/built-with-ai`** — Turn the landing page teaser into a full page from `BUILDING_WITH_AI.md`. Shareable on HN/Reddit. Highlight notable threads with links.
5. **`/docs`** — Hub with subpages: install, config, languages (18 supported), FAQ. Reuse content from README/docs.

#### Stretch ideas
6. **`/performance`** — Cold start numbers, idle CPU stats, benchmark commands. "No Electron" receipts.
7. **`/compare`** — Respectful positioning vs VS Code/Zed/Sublime.

**Nav tip:** Don't clutter the navbar — add `Showcase` and `Docs` as top-level tabs, put changelog/roadmap/AI under a "More" dropdown or in the footer links.

Want me to start building any of these?

Fixed by creating per-cursor primitives and all-cursor wrappers.',\n    lesson: 'Per-item primitives + wrappers scale reliably from single to multi.',\n  },\n  {\n    title: 'CSV Spreadsheet Mode',\n    id: 'T-019b22bd-974e-72e4-9c1e-86526b4b55d0',\n    type: 'Feature',\n    summary: 'A complete spreadsheet UI for CSV/TSV files built across 6 sessions: RFC 4180 parsing, grid rendering with column headers, arrow key navigation, Tab/Shift+Tab cell movement, inline cell editing with proper CSV escaping, and Enter to confirm + move down.',\n    lesson: 'Complex features work best as phased builds with working software at each step.',\n  },\n  {\n    title: 'Unified Text Editing System',\n    id: 'T-019b3643-79af-7573-bb69-18dac832eb94',\n    type: 'Feature',\n    summary: 'Five separate text editing implementations (main editor, command palette, go-to-line, find/replace, CSV cells) with duplicated logic. Solved by creating a unified EditableState<B: TextBuffer> abstraction that shares editing logic across all contexts. All modals gained full cursor navigation, selection, and clipboard support.',\n    lesson: 'Abstract buffer operations behind traits to share editing logic across all contexts.',\n  },\n];\n\nconst modes = [\n  { name: 'Build', purpose: 'New behavior that didn\\'t exist', example: '\"Implement split view (Phase 3)\"', color: 'var(--syn-string)' },\n  { name: 'Improve', purpose: 'Better architecture, same behavior', example: '\"Extract modules from main.rs\"', color: 'var(--syn-function)' },\n  { name: 'Sweep', purpose: 'Fix a cluster of related bugs', example: '\"Multi-cursor selection bugs\"', color: 'var(--syn-keyword)' },\n];\n\nconst lessons = [\n  { title: 'Structure enables autonomy', desc: 'The more explicit your documentation, the more independently AI agents can work.' },\n  { title: 'Modes prevent scope creep', desc: 'Declaring Build/Improve/Sweep at session start keeps both human and AI focused.' },\n  { title: 'Gap docs are underrated', desc: 'When a feature is \"mostly done,\" a gap document transforms fuzzy incompleteness into a concrete checklist.' },\n  { title: 'Review docs before code', desc: 'Having AI review reference documentation catches algorithmic bugs before they become code bugs.' },\n  { title: 'Per-item primitives scale', desc: 'When extending single-item code to multi-item, the \"primitive + wrapper\" pattern works reliably.' },\n];\n---\n\n<SiteLayout title=\"Built with AI — Token\" description=\"How Token was built through 116+ conversations with AI coding assistants. ```
The `Workspace` struct and its associated file tree are defined in `src/model/workspace.rs`. Sidebar rendering is primarily handled in `src/view/mod.rs` with hit testing in `src/view/hit_test.rs`.

### **Workspace & File Tree Model**
- **Workspace Struct**: Manages the sidebar's state including visibility, width, and the file tree.
    - `sidebar_visible: bool`: Controls whether the sidebar is shown.
    - `sidebar_width_logical: f32`: Stores the unscaled width (converted to physical via `sidebar_width()` method).
    - `file_tree: FileTree`: Holds the cached `FileNode` hierarchy.
    - `expanded_folders: HashSet<PathBuf>`: Tracks which directories are currently open.
    - `scroll_offset: usize`: Vertical scroll position in terms of item rows.
- **FileTree & FileNode**:
    - `FileTree`: Scans directories recursively (up to `MAX_DEPTH = 20`) and filters files using `IGNORE_PATTERNS` (e.g., `.git`, `node_modules`).
    - `FileNode`: Represents a file or directory, storing name, path, and children.

### **Initialization & Rendering**
- **Initialization**: Triggered in `src/runtime/app.rs` when a workspace root is provided. It calls `model.open_workspace(root)`, which creates a new `Workspace` and initializes a `FileSystemWatcher`.
- **Rendering**: Handled by `render_sidebar` in `src/view/mod.rs`. It recursively traverses the `FileTree`, drawing nodes if they are within the visible `scroll_offset` and viewport height.
- **Metrics**: Default values like `BASE_SIDEBAR_DEFAULT_WIDTH (250.0)`, `BASE_FILE_TREE_ROW_HEIGHT (22.0)`, and `BASE_FILE_TREE_INDENT (16.0)` are defined in `src/model/mod.rs` within `ScaledMetrics`.

### **Relevant Files**
- **Model Definition**: [src/model/workspace.rs#L559-L606](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/workspace.rs#L559-L606)
- **File Tree Structure**: [src/model/workspace.rs#L215-L244](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/workspace.rs#L215-L244)
- **Rendering Logic**: [src/view/mod.rs#L860-L1049](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L860-L1049)
- **Message Handling (Toggle/Resize)**: [src/update/workspace.rs#L12-L100](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/workspace.rs#L12-L100)
- **Hit Testing (Clicks)**: [src/view/hit_test.rs#L412-L445](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/hit_test.rs#L412-L445)
- **Default Metrics**: [src/model/mod.rs#L278-L320](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/mod.rs#L278-L320)
- **App Startup**: [src/runtime/app.rs#L92-L112](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L92-L112)

```
The command palette and other modals are managed through the `UiState` and `ModalState` types. The search query and filtering for the command palette are handled dynamically during rendering and update cycles rather than being stored as static fields in the state.

### Model Representation
- **Open State**: Controlled by `UiState::active_modal`, which is an `Option<ModalState>`. If `Some`, the modal is open and the `UiState::focus` is set to `FocusTarget::Modal`.
- **Command Palette State**: Defined by `CommandPaletteState` in `src/model/ui.rs`.
- **Search Query**: Stored in `CommandPaletteState::editable`, which is an `EditableState<StringBuffer>`. This holds the text and cursor/selection state for the input field.
- **Filtered Results**: These are **not stored** in the `CommandPaletteState`. Instead, they are computed on-the-fly using `crate::commands::filter_commands(&input_text)` during both rendering (`src/view/mod.rs`) and update logic (`src/update/ui.rs`).

Relevant files:
- [src/model/ui.rs#L81-L109](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/ui.rs#L81-L109) - `CommandPaletteState` definition and helpers.
- [src/model/ui.rs#L324-L344](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/ui.rs#L324-L344) - `ModalState` enum combining all modal types.
- [src/model/ui.rs#L444-L485](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/ui.rs#L444-L485) - `UiState` containing the `active_modal` field.

### Rendering
Modals are rendered in `src/view/mod.rs` via the `render_modals` function. It performs the following steps:
1.  **Dimming**: Dims the background using `frame.dim(0x66)`.
2.  **Layout**: Uses `geometry::modal_bounds` to calculate centered coordinates.
3.  **Input Field**: Renders the search box using `TextFieldRenderer::render`.
4.  **Results List**: Calls `filter_commands` and iterates through the results, highlighting the item at `state.selected_index`.

Relevant files:
- [src/view/mod.rs#L1842-L2058](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L1842-L2058) - The `render_modals` implementation, specifically the `CommandPalette` match arm.
- [src/view/geometry.rs#L824-L851](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/geometry.rs#L824-L851) - `modal_bounds` calculation logic.
```

```
The `Renderer` in `src/view/mod.rs` uses a set of `render_*` methods to draw the UI components. While the top-level `render` method is public, most specific component renderers are private, with a few exceptions like `render_status_bar`.

### Public Render Methods
- **`render`**: The main entry point that orchestrates the entire frame rendering.
  - `pub fn render(&mut self, model: &mut AppModel, perf: &mut crate::perf::PerfStats, damage: &Damage) -> Result<()>` [L2307-L2312](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L2307-L2312)
- **`render_status_bar`**: Draws the bottom info bar including modes, file info, and cursor position.
  - `pub fn render_status_bar(frame: &mut Frame, painter: &mut TextPainter, model: &AppModel, window_width: usize, window_height: usize)` [L1790-L1796](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L1790-L1796)

### Internal Component Renderers
- **`render_editor_area`**: Handles the main workspace, including editor groups and splitters.
  - `fn render_editor_area(frame: &mut Frame, painter: &mut TextPainter, model: &AppModel, splitters: &[SplitterBar])` [L506-L511](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L506-L511)
- **`render_sidebar`**: Draws the file tree/project explorer.
  - `fn render_sidebar(frame: &mut Frame, painter: &mut TextPainter, model: &AppModel, sidebar_width: usize, sidebar_height: usize)` [L861-L867](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L861-L867)
- **`render_modals`**: A dispatcher for various overlay dialogs (Command Palette, Theme Picker, Goto Line, etc.).
  - `fn render_modals(frame: &mut Frame, painter: &mut TextPainter, model: &AppModel, window_width: usize, window_height: usize)` [L1842-L1848](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L1842-L1848)
- **`render_csv_grid`**: Specialized renderer for tabular data.
  - `fn render_csv_grid(frame: &mut Frame, painter: &mut TextPainter, model: &AppModel, csv: &crate::csv::CsvState, layout: &geometry::GroupLayout, is_focused: bool)` [L1489-L1496](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L1489-L1496)
- **`render_drop_overlay`**: Visual feedback for file drag-and-drop operations.
  - `fn render_drop_overlay(frame: &mut Frame, painter: &mut TextPainter, model: &AppModel, window_width: usize, window_height: usize)` [L2272-L2278](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L2272-L2278)
- **`render_dock`**: Handles side and bottom panels (e.g., terminal, output).
  - `fn render_dock(frame: &mut Frame, painter: &mut TextPainter, model: &AppModel, position: crate::panel::DockPosition, rect: crate::model::editor_area::Rect)` [L2539-L2545](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L2539-L2545)
- **`render_cursor_lines_only`**: Optimized path for redrawing only lines with active cursors.
  - `fn render_cursor_lines_only(frame: &mut Frame, painter: &mut TextPainter, model: &AppModel, dirty_lines: &[usize])` [L213-L218](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L213-L218)

## TL;DR
The highest “marketing value per LOC” upgrades are: **(1) render sidebar correctly (workspace + layout rect adjustment), (2) render + configure modals, (3) add selections**, then **(4) real tab bars**. Mixed/nested splits are nice, but are the most invasive because the current YAML/schema is “flat list of files → N splits”.

---

## Recommended approach (simple path)

### 0) Fix the renderer integration first (required for sidebar + modals)
Right now `render_to_buffer()` always renders only the editor area + splitters + status bar, using a full-window `available_rect`. To make *any* sidebar/modal work, do both:

1) **Account for sidebar width when computing the editor rect**
```rust
let status_bar_height = font_info.line_height as f32;

let sidebar_width = model.workspace.as_ref()
  .filter(|ws| ws.sidebar_visible)
  .map(|ws| ws.sidebar_width(model.metrics.scale_factor)) // or scenario.scale
  .unwrap_or(0.0);

let available_rect = Rect::new(
  sidebar_width,
  0.0,
  width as f32 - sidebar_width,
  height as f32 - status_bar_height,
);
```

2) **Actually render sidebar + modals**
You likely already have renderer entrypoints similar to `render_sidebar(...)` / `render_modal(...)` / `render_dock(...)` in `token::view::Renderer`. If they exist, call them in the same order as the real app render pipeline, e.g.:

- draw background
- draw sidebar (if visible)
- draw editor area + splitters
- draw dock panels (if any)
- draw status bar
- draw modal last (on top)

If you *don’t* have these functions exposed, the simplest enhancement is to add a single `Renderer::render_app(frame, painter, model)` that the real editor uses, and have screenshot call that (prevents future drift).

**Worth it for showcase screenshots:** **Yes (must-have)**. Without this, the rest won’t ever appear.

Effort: **M (1–3h)** depending on whether there’s already a top-level render function.

---

## Gap 1: Sidebar / workspace

### YAML schema addition
Add an optional `workspace` block:

```yaml
workspace:
  root: "."                # path on disk
  sidebar:
    visible: true
    width_px: 420          # physical pixels (stable for screenshots)
    scroll_offset: 0
    expanded:
      - "src"
      - "src/model"
      - "assets"
    selected: "src/bin/screenshot.rs"   # highlights in tree
    reveal_open_file: true              # convenience default
```

Notes:
- Use **relative paths** under `root` for `expanded/selected`, because the tool will canonicalize root anyway.
- `width_px` should be physical pixels (since screenshots are pixel-based and you already operate in physical `width/height`).

### `create_model_from_scenario` implementation
1) After `model` construction, create a workspace:
```rust
if let Some(ws_scn) = &scenario.workspace {
    let mut ws = Workspace::new(ws_scn.root.clone(), &model.metrics)?;
    ws.sidebar_visible = ws_scn.sidebar.visible;
    ws.set_sidebar_width(ws_scn.sidebar.width_px as f32, scenario.scale);
    ws.scroll_offset = ws_scn.sidebar.scroll_offset;

    // Expand folders
    for rel in &ws_scn.sidebar.expanded {
        ws.expand_folder(&ws.root.join(rel));
    }

    // Select/reveal
    if ws_scn.sidebar.reveal_open_file {
        if let Some(doc) = model.editor_area.focused_document() {
            if let Some(path) = &doc.file_path { ws.reveal_file(path); }
        }
    }
    if let Some(sel) = &ws_scn.sidebar.selected {
        ws.reveal_file(&ws.root.join(sel));
    }

    model.workspace = Some(ws);
}
```

2) In `render_to_buffer`, compute sidebar width and shift `available_rect` as described above.

**Worth it for showcase screenshots:** **Very high**. The file tree instantly makes screenshots “real IDE-like”.

Effort: **M (1–3h)**.

**Guardrail:** Workspace scanning can be slow/variable if `root` is huge. For reproducible marketing shots, point `root` at a small curated directory (e.g., `screenshots/fixtures/project/`).

---

## Gap 2: Modals (command palette, find/replace, goto line, theme picker, file finder)

### YAML schema addition
Add an optional `modal` block (and keep it narrow / explicit so scenarios stay readable):

```yaml
modal:
  id: "command_palette"      # command_palette | goto_line | find_replace | theme_picker | file_finder
  command_palette:
    input: "theme"
    selected_index: 1

# or
modal:
  id: "find_replace"
  find_replace:
    query: "render_to_buffer"
    replacement: "render_app_to_buffer"
    replace_mode: true
    case_sensitive: false
    focused_field: "query"    # query | replace
```

### `create_model_from_scenario` implementation
- Construct the corresponding state type from `ui.rs` and then set it via `model.ui.open_modal(...)`.

The key is how `ModalState` is defined (not shown in the snippet). The simplest implementation pattern:

```rust
match scenario.modal.id {
  CommandPalette => {
    let mut st = CommandPaletteState::default();
    st.set_input(&scenario.modal.command_palette.input);
    st.selected_index = scenario.modal.command_palette.selected_index;
    model.ui.open_modal(ModalState::CommandPalette(st));
  }
  FindReplace => {
    let mut st = FindReplaceState::default();
    st.set_query(&scenario.modal.find_replace.query);
    st.set_replacement(&scenario.modal.find_replace.replacement);
    st.replace_mode = scenario.modal.find_replace.replace_mode;
    st.case_sensitive = scenario.modal.find_replace.case_sensitive;
    st.focused_field = ...;
    model.ui.open_modal(ModalState::FindReplace(st));
  }
  ...
}
```

**Important rendering side:** ensure `render_to_buffer()` calls the modal renderer last.

**Worth it for showcase screenshots:** **High**. Modals add “action” and visual interest with minimal scene complexity.

Effort: **S–M (<1h–3h)** if `ModalState` is accessible; **M** if you need to expose constructors/enum variants.

**Guardrail:** Some modals (notably file finder) may expect precomputed results/matches. If it looks empty, either:
- accept “empty state” for screenshots, or
- add a YAML field to inject fake matches (advanced; likely not worth it initially).

---

## Gap 3: Mixed / nested split directions

### YAML schema addition (pragmatic, minimal)
Introduce a recursive layout tree and keep your existing flat `files:` as a convenience mode.

Example:

```yaml
layout:
  split:
    direction: "horizontal"
    children:
      - group:
          tabs:
            - { path: "src/main.rs", active: true }
      - split:
          direction: "vertical"
          children:
            - group: { tabs: [ { path: "src/bin/screenshot.rs", active: true } ] }
            - group: { tabs: [ { path: "README.md", active: true } ] }
```

Optional later: `ratios: [0.55, 0.45]`.

### `create_model_from_scenario` implementation
**Simple approach:** don’t try to drive this via repeated `update(Msg::Layout(...))` unless the layout system supports “focus node by path” and “split specific group”. Instead:

- Add a “builder” in the screenshot tool that constructs `EditorArea` directly from the tree (groups, editors, documents).
- Then set `model.editor_area = built_editor_area`.

This avoids a long sequence of layout messages and fragile “focus juggling”.

**Worth it for showcase screenshots:** **Medium**. Nice for “power-user” visuals, but you can get 80% of the marketing value with 2–3 uniform splits plus tabs + sidebar + modal.

Effort: **L (1–2d)** depending on how `EditorArea` is structured internally.

---

## Gap 4: Selections (highlighted ranges)

### YAML schema addition
Add selections per open tab/editor. Start simple: a list of ranges in (line, col) space.

```yaml
files:
  - path: "src/bin/screenshot.rs"
    cursor_line: 326
    cursor_column: 4
    selections:
      - start: { line: 326, column: 4 }
        end:   { line: 326, column: 20 }
      - start: { line: 336, column: 8 }
        end:   { line: 341, column: 6 }
```

### `create_model_from_scenario` implementation
Where you currently call `apply_cursor_and_scroll(editor, file)`, extend it:

- Parse `selections` into your editor selection type (not shown here; likely something like `Selection { anchor, active }`).
- Set `editor.selections = ...` and ensure the primary cursor matches the “active end” of the primary selection.

If selections are stored separately from cursors, set both consistently.

**Worth it for showcase screenshots:** **High**. Selections communicate capability immediately (multi-cursor + selections looks “pro”).

Effort: **S–M (<1h–3h)** depending on selection struct exposure.

---

## Gap 5: File tabs (multiple tabs per group)

### YAML schema addition
This fits naturally into the same “layout tree” concept, but you can also add a smaller incremental schema if you keep current split model:

**Incremental schema (works with today’s `files:`):**
```yaml
groups:
  - tabs:
      - { path: "src/bin/screenshot.rs", active: true }
      - { path: "src/model/ui.rs" }
  - tabs:
      - { path: "README.md", active: true }
split_direction: "vertical"
```

(Where `groups.len()` determines how many splits; each group gets a tab bar.)

### `create_model_from_scenario` implementation
This depends on how tabs are represented (not shown). You likely have:
- a “group” that contains multiple editors/tabs, and
- an “active tab/editor id” per group.

Implementation options:
1) **Preferred:** construct `EditorArea` groups + tabs directly (like the nested split builder, but only one split level).
2) **Fallback:** if there are layout/editor messages like “OpenFileInFocusedGroup” / “NewTab” / “FocusTabIndex”, you can `update()` them.

**Worth it for showcase screenshots:** **Very high**. Tabs are one of the first things people expect in an editor screenshot.

Effort: **M–L (1–2d)** depending on internal structures/messages.

---

## Risks and guardrails
- **Renderer drift:** If screenshot rendering calls low-level pieces (editor area only), it will keep missing UI. Add/route through a single “app render” function to stay aligned.
- **Workspace scanning variance:** Large roots will slow renders and create unstable visuals. Use a curated fixtures directory.
- **Modal internal coupling:** Some modals may assume derived data (filtered commands, fuzzy matches). Start by rendering the modal chrome + input; only simulate results if empty looks bad.
- **Schema bloat:** Keep the YAML additions orthogonal (workspace, modal, layout/tabs, selections). Don’t overfit to every UI state.

---

## When to consider the advanced path
Move to a fully general “layout tree builder” (nested splits + tabs + focus) when:
- you need screenshots matching complex real-world layouts (3+ panes with different orientations), or
- you want to showcase “workspace + file finder + preview + docks” in one scene.

---

## Optional advanced path (outline)
- Add `layout:` recursive tree (splits/groups).
- Add a “virtual workspace tree” option in YAML to avoid disk scanning (deterministic marketing shots).
- Add “fake file finder results” injection for polished modal screenshots.
```

*   **Status Indicators:** **Default Dark** and **Fleet Dark** both have green checkmarks to their right, likely indicating previously active or currently applied states.

#### Layout and Appearance
*   **Position:** A floating rectangular overlay positioned in the upper-center of the editor.
*   **Styling:** Dark grey background with a thin border.
*   **Typography:**
    *   **Switch Theme:** Main title at the top.
    *   **Built-in Themes:** Sub-header in a dimmer, greyed-out font.

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01K6dttoACH4HCebcg2FHVzL`

```
### Selection Highlighting Analysis

**Size and Shape**
- The selection is a rectangular "block" or "column" selection spanning lines 20, 21, and 22.
- It covers approximately the first 8-10 characters of horizontal space, encompassing the leading indentation and the initial characters of `name:`, `age,`, and `email:`.
- A prominent white vertical cursor bar is attached to the left edge of the selection on line 20.

**Color and Visibility**
- **Color:** A semi-transparent dark blue/indigo overlay.
- **Visibility:** Low contrast against the dark background. While the text remains legible, the selection does not "pop." The primary visual indicator is the white cursor line rather than the highlighted area itself.

**Reasons for Poor Aesthetics**
- **Muddied Syntax Highlighting:** The blue overlay desaturates the syntax colors underneath (e.g., the orange/tan of the variable names), making the code look "dirty" rather than highlighted.
- **Inconsistent Alignment:** Because it is a block selection, it creates a rigid box that ignores the natural flow and length of the code lines.
- **Lack of Definition:** There is no border or subtle glow to define the boundaries of the selection area, making it look like a flat, dull alpha-mask.
- **Visual Weight:** The white cursor bar on the left is significantly higher in contrast than the selection box itself, drawing the eye to the edge rather than the selected content.
```

## Assistant

Here's a summary of everything done:

### Screenshot Tool Enhancements

#### New capabilities added to `src/bin/screenshot.rs`:

**1. Sidebar/Workspace** — New `workspace:` YAML block:
```yaml
workspace:
  root: "."
  sidebar_visible: true
  sidebar_width: 250       # logical pixels
  expanded: [src, src/model]
  selected: src/main.rs
```

**2. Modals** — New `modal:` YAML block supporting all 4 modal types:
```yaml
modal:
  id: command_palette | goto_line | find_replace | theme_picker
  input: "search text"          # command palette / goto line
  selected_index: 2             # palette / theme picker
  query: "fn "                  # find/replace
  replacement: "pub fn "        # find/replace
```

**3. Selections** — New `selections:` per file for highlighted text ranges:
```yaml
selections:
  - anchor_line: 10
    anchor_column: 8
    head_line: 10
    head_column: 12
```

**4. Renderer pipeline fix** — `render_to_buffer` now matches the real app render order: editor → splitters → sidebar → status bar → modals.

#### Updated screenshots:
- **`showcase-selection`** — Now shows 5 word-level selections of `"name"` spread across the file (simulates ⌘+J workflow)
- **`showcase-command-palette`** — Now shows the theme picker modal with Catppuccin Mocha highlighted

#### 7 new scenario files:
`showcase-sidebar`, `showcase-sidebar-splits`, `showcase-command-palette` (theme picker), `showcase-find-replace`, `showcase-theme-picker`, `showcase-selection`, `showcase-goto-line`
